Um, there was a time not too long ago where I just had this sudden burst of energy. I couldn't seem to sit still after my shift at the store. I'd come home and just keep going... sketching out new ideas for the displays, reorganizing all my own shelves at home. I even signed up for some extra management training courses that I didn't really need. I just felt like I had to keep moving, keep doing things.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I might have stayed up a bit later than usual because I was so focused on those display ideas, but I still went to bed. I didn't notice needing a lot less sleep or anything like that. If I didn't get my normal amount, I definitely still felt tired the next day at the store. So, no, my sleep didn't really change all that much.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think so. I mean, I've had stressful days at the store, or times when I felt a bit down, but... never anything like this. This is really the first time I've felt this low for weeks at a time.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it actually did. Um... this whole up-and-down stretch with my mood and energy has been going on for about two months now. That's right around the time when the trouble with our new district manager really started.\n\nDoctor: When the problems with the new district manager ease up, does your mood tend to improve?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. The problems haven't actually eased up at all since she took over, so... I haven't really had a chance to see if my mood would bounce back. It's just been a pretty constant issue at work every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I'm still eating about the same as I usually do. So... my appetite seems pretty normal, even with everything else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping more or less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep has been about the same. I guess... I still get my usual amount of rest each night. I haven't been staying in bed longer or waking up in the middle of the night, so that part seems pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I just felt like I had to keep moving, keep doing things.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did you need much less sleep than usual and still feel rested?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I might have stayed up a bit later than usual because I was so focused on those display ideas, but I still went to bed. I didn't notice needing a lot less sleep or anything like that. If I didn't get my normal amount, I definitely still felt tired the next day at the store. So, no, my sleep didn't really change all that much.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't think so. I mean, I've had stressful days at the store, or times when I felt a bit down, but... never anything like this. This is really the first time I've felt this low for weeks at a time.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after something stressful happened in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, it actually did. Um... this whole up-and-down stretch with my mood and energy has been going on for about two months now. That's right around the time when the trouble with our new district manager really started.\n\nDoctor: When the problems with the new district manager ease up, does your mood tend to improve?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. The problems haven't actually eased up at all since she took over, so... I haven't really had a chance to see if my mood would bounce back. It's just been a pretty constant issue at work every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I usually really enjoy my art projects, and I still set out my paints, or I'll think about going to my pottery class... but when I actually sit down to do it, I just stare at the materials. I just feel none of the spark I used to feel.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. Most mornings lately, I wake up with this... just a heavy, gray feeling. It's like all the color has drained out of everything before I even get dressed to head into the store.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this down nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's definitely been that way for at least two weeks. Honestly, it feels like it's been going on for a month or more, just... every single day, waking up with that same heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any change there. I'm still eating about the same as I usually do. So... my appetite seems pretty normal, even with everything else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping more or less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep has been about the same. I guess... I still get my usual amount of rest each night. I haven't been staying in bed longer or waking up in the middle of the night, so that part seems pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. It's mostly with making decisions. Things that used to be completely automatic for me, like choosing which endcap to reset first at work, or even just figuring out what blouse to wear in the morning... I'll just end up standing there feeling foggy. I keep second-guessing myself on things I shouldn't even have to think twice about.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been making it hard for you to function at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I mean, I'm still getting to work every day, and I'm taking care of myself at home just fine. So I am functioning. But... it is definitely having an impact. I've started avoiding extra conversations with my staff on the floor. And I find myself putting off those simple store decisions I mentioned earlier, just because... well, I really don't trust my own judgment right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I suppose I have. I find myself... well, replaying little mistakes at work over and over. Just minor things, really, like leaving a display sign slightly crooked, or noticing one of my trainees looking confused. But when that happens, I just start telling myself that I’m a completely useless supervisor. It's been hard to shake that feeling lately.\n\nDoctor: Do your feelings lately seem much stronger than what you’d normally expect from the problems with your new district manager?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, things with the new district manager have definitely been stressful, but... I haven't really thought about whether my feelings are stronger than they should be. It's hard to say what a normal reaction to it would even look like right now. I just know this is how I've been feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings seem out of proportion to what most people would feel in response to the problems with your new district manager?\n\nPatient: I guess... well, like I was just saying, I really don't know. It's hard for me to judge how most people would handle it. I haven't really compared my reaction to what anyone else would do... I just know how heavy it's all been feeling for me personally.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
